**Croatia**,[^cite_note-10] officially the **Republic of Croatia**,[^cite_note-11] is a country in [Central](5188.md) and [Southeastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeastern_Europe), on the coast of the [Adriatic Sea](23275478.md). It borders [Slovenia](27338.md) to the northwest, [Hungary](13275.md) to the northeast, [Serbia](29265.md) to the east, [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) and [Montenegro](20760.md) to the southeast, and shares a maritime border with [Italy](14532.md) to the west. The [Croatian archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_inhabited_islands_of_Croatia) contains over 1,000 islands and islets, the [largest overseas territory](23275478.md#Geography) on the Adriatic Sea. Its capital, largest city and main cultural and economic centre is [Zagreb](55906.md). Major urban centers include [Split](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Split,_Croatia), [Rijeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rijeka), and [Osijek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osijek). The country is composed of [twenty counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counties_of_Croatia)[^cite_note-12] spanning 56,594 square kilometres (21,851 square miles) within [four administrative regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regions_of_Croatia). Croatia has [a population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Croatia) of nearly 3.9 million as of 2026.&#x20;

[Croatian history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Croatia) began with the 6th century arrival of the [Croats](69801.md) to [Roman Illyria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyria). Settled as [two duchies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Croatia) a century later, Croatia was granted independence in 878 under Duke [Branimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branimir,_Duke_of_Croatia) and elevated into [a kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(925%E2%80%931102\)) under King [Tomislav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomislav,_King_of_Croatia) in 925. The [Trpimirović dynastic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trpimirovi%C4%87_dynasty) succession crisis [placed Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_in_personal_union_with_Hungary) into a [personal union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_union) with [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_\(1000%E2%80%931301\)) in 1102. In 1527, amid [Ottoman conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hundred_Years%27_Croatian%E2%80%93Ottoman_War), Croatia aligned with the [Habsburg monarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy). It reorganized into the [State of Slovenes, Croats, and Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs) before [merging into](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creation_of_Yugoslavia) the [Kingdom of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia) in 1918. During [World War II](32927.md), most of Croatia was invaded by the [Axis powers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis_powers) who installed a [puppet state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_War_II_puppet_states) known as the [Independent State of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia). A [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Partisans) restored sovereignty, emerging as the [Socialist Republic of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Croatia) within the [Socialist Federal Republic of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia) in 1943. Croatia declared [independence from Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Croatia) in 1991, successfully fighting the [War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_War_of_Independence) until 1995.&#x20;

Modern Croatia is a [representative democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy) with a [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Croatia), [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Croatia), and [unicameral parliament](398696.md). Its [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Croatia) ensures separation of powers between the [executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Croatia), [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Croatia), and [judicial branches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Croatia). [Croatian politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Croatia) reflect [a multi-party system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Croatia). Croatia is part of many international organizations and forums including the [European Union](9317.md), [United Nations](31769.md), [WTO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), and [NATO](21133.md), among others. It [exerts regional influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Croatia) as a [small power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_power) in Europe, particularly within neighboring Southeast Europe. Since 1999, the [Croatian Armed Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Croatia) have deployed internationally for a variety of peacekeeping missions and military interventions. Croatia [was elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_United_Nations_Security_Council_election) to serve on the non-permanent seat of the [UN Security Council](31956.md) from 2008 to 2009.&#x20;

A [developed country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country), Croatia has [an advanced economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Croatia) considered [high-income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High-income) and dominated by its service, industrial, and agricultural sectors. Its [tourism sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Croatia) is prominent, generating significant economic activity across the country. Croatia has heavily invested in infrastructure, especially in [transport routes and facilities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Croatia) along [European corridors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-European_corridors). An [emerging energy power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Croatia) in Europe, it has strategic investments in [liquefied natural gas](832128.md), [geothermal networks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_energy), and electric transport. It has extensive biodiversity, wildlife, and [natural resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Croatia) across [its geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Croatia). Croatia provides [social security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_spending), [universal health care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Croatia), and subsidized [primary and secondary education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Croatia) while supporting [culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Croatia) through [media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Croatia) and [publishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_literature).&#x20;

## Etymology

Main article: [Names of the Croats and Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Croats_and_Croatia)

Croatia's [non-native](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endonym_and_exonym) [name](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Croats_and_Croatia) derives from [Medieval Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medieval_Latin) *Croātia*, itself a derivation of [North-West Slavic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Slavic_languages) \**Xərwate*, by [liquid metathesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavic_liquid_metathesis_and_pleophony) from Common Slavic period *\*Xorvat*, from proposed [Proto-Slavic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proto-Slavic_language) *[\*Xъrvátъ](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Reconstruction\:Proto-Slavic/x%D1%8Arvat%D1%8A)* which possibly comes from the 3rd-century [Scytho-Sarmatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scythian_languages) form attested in the [Tanais Tablets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanais_Tablets) as Χοροάθος (*_Khoroáthos_*. Alternate forms are *Khoróatos* and *_Khoroúathos_*).[^cite_note-Gluhak-1993-13] The origin of the ethnonym is uncertain, but most probably is from [Proto-Ossetian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ossetian_language) / [Alanian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scythian_languages#Alanian) \**xurvæt-* or \**xurvāt-*, in the meaning of "one who guards" ("guardian, protector").[^cite_note-14]&#x20;

The oldest preserved record of the Croatian ethnonym's native variation *\*xъrvatъ* is of the variable stem, attested in the [Baška tablet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ba%C5%A1ka_tablet) in [style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Style_\(form_of_address\)) zvъnъmirъ kralъ xrъvatъskъ ("[Zvonimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demetrius_Zvonimir_of_Croatia), [Croatian king](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Croatia)"),[^cite_note-Fučić-1971-15] while the Latin variation *Croatorum* is archaeologically confirmed on a church inscription found in [Bijaći](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bija%C4%87i) near [Trogir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trogir) dated to the end of the 8th or early 9th century.[^cite_note-16] The presumably oldest stone [inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epigraphy) with fully preserved ethnonym is the 9th-century [Branimir inscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branimir_inscription) found near [Benkovac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benkovac), where Duke [Branimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branimir_of_Croatia) is styled *Dux Cruatorvm*, likely dated between 879 and 892, during his rule.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić2007195–198-17] The Latin term *_Chroatorum_* is attributed to a charter of Duke [Trpimir I of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trpimir_I_of_Croatia), dated to 852 in a 1568 copy of a lost original, but it is not certain if the original was found to be older than the Branimir inscription.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić200727-18][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić2007171-19]&#x20;

## History

Main article: [History of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Croatia)

### Prehistory and antiquity

Main article: [History of Croatia before the Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Croatia_before_the_Croats)

Ceramic sculpture

Stone Sculpture

Left: The [Vučedol dove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vu%C4%8Dedol_dove), a sculpture from 2800 to 2500 BC.\
Right: *[Croatian Apoxyomenos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Apoxyomenos)*, [Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greece) statue, 2nd or 1st century BC.

The area known as modern Croatia was inhabited throughout the [prehistoric period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric_period). [Neanderthal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neanderthal) fossils dating to the middle [Palaeolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palaeolithic) period were unearthed in northern Croatia, best presented at the [Krapina site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krapina_Neanderthal_site).[^cite_note-20] Remnants of [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) and [Chalcolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chalcolithic) cultures were found in all regions.[^cite_note-21] The largest proportion of sites is in the valleys of northern Croatia. The most significant are [Baden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baden_culture), [Starčevo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star%C4%8Devo_culture), and [Vučedol cultures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vu%C4%8Dedol_culture).[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23] [Iron Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Age) hosted the early Illyrian [Hallstatt culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hallstatt_culture) and the Celtic [La Tène culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_T%C3%A8ne_culture).[^cite_note-24]&#x20;

The region of modern-day Croatia was settled by [Illyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrians) and [Liburnians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liburnians), while the first Greek colonies were established on the islands of [Hvar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hvar),[^cite_note-25] [Korčula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kor%C4%8Dula), and [Vis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vis_\(island\)).[^cite_note-26] In 9 AD, the territory of today's Croatia became part of the [Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire). Emperor [Diocletian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocletian) was native to the region. He [had a large palace built](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocletian%27s_Palace) in [Split](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Split,_Croatia), to which he retired after abdicating in AD 305.[^cite_note-27] During the 5th century, the [last de jure Western Roman Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Last_de_jure_Western_Roman_Emperor) [Julius Nepos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julius_Nepos) ruled a small realm from the palace after fleeing Italy in 475.[^cite_note-28]&#x20;

### Middle Ages

Main articles: [Duchy of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Croatia), [Kingdom of Croatia (925–1102)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(925%E2%80%931102\)), [Kingdom of Croatia (1102–1526)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(1102%E2%80%931526\)), and [Republic of Ragusa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Ragusa)



[Kingdom of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(925%E2%80%931102\)) c. 925, during the reign of [King Tomislav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Tomislav)

The Roman period ends with [Avar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avars_\(Carpathians\)) and [Croat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croat) invasions in the late 6th and first half of the 7th century and the destruction of almost all Roman towns. Roman survivors retreated to more favourable sites on the coast, islands, and mountains. The city of [Dubrovnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubrovnik) was founded by such survivors from [Epidaurum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidaurum).[^cite_note-AAPatton-29]&#x20;

The [ethnogenesis of the Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Origin_hypotheses_of_the_Croats) is a subject of scholarly discussion, reflecting the complexity of early medieval migrations and the integration of diverse populations. The predominant scholarly consensus, based on historical chronicles, proposes that the [White Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Croats) originated as a Slavic group that migrated from [White Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Croatia) during the [Migration Period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migration_Period). Conversely, the Iranian theory proposes [Sarmatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarmatian)-[Alanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alans) origin of Proto-Croats, based on [Tanais Tablets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanais_Tablets) containing [Ancient Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greek) inscriptions of given names *Χορούαθος*, *Χοροάθος*, and *Χορόαθος* (Khoroúathos, Khoroáthos, and Khoróathos) and their interpretation as [anthroponyms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthroponym) related to the [Croatian ethnonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_of_the_Croats_and_Croatia).[^cite_note-Nikšić-Heršak-30]&#x20;

According to the work *[De Administrando Imperio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Administrando_Imperio)* written by 10th-century Byzantine Emperor [Constantine VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constantine_VII), [Croats](69801.md) settled in the [Roman province of Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_province_of_Dalmatia) in the first half of the 7th century after they defeated the [Avars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pannonian_Avars).[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldstein199913-32][^cite_note-33] Although there exist some scholarly disputes about the account's reliability and interpretation,[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35] recent archaeological data has established that the migration and settlement of the Slavs/Croats was in the late 6th and early 7th century.[^cite_note-Belos00-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38] Eventually, a [dukedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dukedom_\(administrative_division\)) was formed, [Duchy of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duchy_of_Croatia), ruled by [Borna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borna_\(duke\)), as attested by chronicles of [Einhard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einhard) starting in 818. The record represents the first document of Croatian realms, [vassal states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vassal_states) of [Francia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francia) at the time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić2007157–160-39] Its neighbor to the North was [Principality of Lower Pannonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principality_of_Lower_Pannonia), at the time ruled by duke [Ljudevit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ljudevit_\(Lower_Pannonia\)) who ruled the territories between the [Drava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drava) and [Sava](587845.md) rivers, centred from his fort at [Sisak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sisak). This population and territory throughout history was tightly related and connected to Croats and Croatia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBudak2018178–185-40]&#x20;

[Christianisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianisation) of Croats began in the 7th century at the time of archon [Porga of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porga_of_Croatia), initially probably encompassed only the elite and related people,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBudak2018144–145-41] but mostly finished by the 9th century.[^cite_note-42][^cite_note-43] The Frankish overlordship ended during the reign of [Mislav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mislav_of_Croatia),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić2007169–170-44] or his successor [Trpimir I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trpimir_I_of_Croatia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBudak2018106-45] The native Croatian royal dynasty was founded by duke Trpimir I in the mid 9th century, who defeated the Byzantine and Bulgarian forces.[^cite_note-46] The first native Croatian ruler recognised by the Pope was duke [Branimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branimir_of_Croatia), who received papal recognition from [Pope John VIII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_VIII) on 7 June 879.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMužić2007195–198-17] [Tomislav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomislav_of_Croatia) was the first [king of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Croatia), noted as such in a letter of [Pope John X](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_X) in 925. Tomislav defeated Hungarian and Bulgarian invasions.[^cite_note-Posavec-47] The medieval Croatian kingdom reached its peak in the 11th century during the reigns of [Petar Krešimir IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petar_Kre%C5%A1imir_IV) (1058–1074) and [Dmitar Zvonimir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitar_Zvonimir) (1075–1089).[^cite_note-Margetić-48] When [Stjepan II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_II_of_Croatia) died in 1091, ending the Trpimirović dynasty, Dmitar Zvonimir's brother-in-law [Ladislaus I of Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary) claimed the Croatian crown. This led to a [war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Gvozd_Mountain) and [personal union with Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_union_of_Croatia_and_Hungary) in 1102 under [Coloman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coloman,_King_of_Hungary).[^cite_note-HR-HU-Heka-49]&#x20;

### Union with Hungary and Austria

Main articles: [Kingdom of Croatia (Habsburg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(Habsburg\)) and [Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary)

Further information: [Croatian–Ottoman wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian%E2%80%93Ottoman_wars)



Ban [Josip Jelačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josip_Jela%C4%8Di%C4%87) at the opening of the first modern [Croatian Parliament](398696.md) (*Sabor*), 5 June 1848. The Croatian tricolour flag can be seen in the background.

For the next four centuries, the Kingdom of Croatia was ruled by the [Sabor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabor) (parliament) and a [Ban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_of_Croatia) (viceroy) appointed by the king.[^cite_note-Povijest-saborovanja-50] This period saw the rise of influential nobility such as the [Frankopan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankopan) and [Šubić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%A0ubi%C4%87) families to prominence, and ultimately numerous Bans from the two families.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFont200517-51] An increasing threat of [Ottoman](22278.md) conquest and a struggle against the [Republic of Venice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Venice) for control of coastal areas ensued. The Venetians controlled most of Dalmatia by 1428, except the [city-state of Dubrovnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City-state_of_Dubrovnik), which became independent. [Ottoman conquests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hundred_Years%27_Croatian%E2%80%93Ottoman_War) led to the 1493 [Battle of Krbava field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Krbava_field) and the 1526 [Battle of Mohács](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Moh%C3%A1cs), both ending in decisive Ottoman victories. King [Louis II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_II_of_Hungary) died at Mohács, and in 1527, the [Croatian Parliament met in Cetin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Parliament_met_in_Cetin) and chose Ferdinand I of the [House of Habsburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Habsburg) as the new ruler of Croatia, under the condition that he protects Croatia against the Ottoman Empire while respecting its political rights.[^cite_note-Povijest-saborovanja-50][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005422–423-52]&#x20;

Following the decisive Ottoman victories, Croatia was split into civilian and military territories in 1538. The military territories became known as the [Croatian Military Frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Military_Frontier) and were under direct Habsburg control. Ottoman advances in Croatia continued until the 1593 [Battle of Sisak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Sisak), the first decisive Ottoman defeat, when borders stabilised.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005422–423-52] During the [Great Turkish War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Turkish_War) (1683–1698), [Slavonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavonia) was regained, but western [Bosnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnia_\(region\)), which had been part of Croatia before the Ottoman conquest, remained outside Croatian control.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005422–423-52] The present-day border between the two countries is a remnant of this outcome. [Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalmatia), the southern part of the border, was similarly defined by the [Fifth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cretan_War_\(1645%E2%80%931669\)) and the [Seventh Ottoman–Venetian Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seventh_Ottoman%E2%80%93Venetian_Wars).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELane1973409-53]&#x20;

The [Ottoman wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian%E2%80%93Ottoman_wars) drove demographic changes. During the 16th century, Croats from western and northern [Bosnia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnia_\(region\)), [Lika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lika), [Krbava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krbava), the area between the rivers [Una](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Una_\(Sava\)) and [Kupa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupa), and especially from western [Slavonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavonia), migrated towards [Austria](26964606.md). Present-day [Burgenland Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgenland_Croats) are direct descendants of these settlers.[^cite_note-54][^cite_note-55] To replace the fleeing population, the Habsburgs encouraged Bosnians to provide military service in the [Military Frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Frontier). The [Croatian Parliament](398696.md) supported [King Charles III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_Charles_VI)'s [Pragmatic Sanction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pragmatic_Sanction_of_1713) and signed their own [Pragmatic Sanction in 1712](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pragmatic_Sanction_in_1712).[^cite_note-56] Subsequently, the emperor pledged to respect all privileges and political rights of the [Kingdom of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_\(Habsburg\)), and [Queen Maria Theresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Maria_Theresa) made significant contributions to Croatian affairs, such as introducing compulsory education.&#x20;



The [Kingdom of Croatia-Slavonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia-Slavonia) was an autonomous kingdom within [Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary) created in 1868 following the [Croatian–Hungarian Settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian%E2%80%93Hungarian_Settlement).

Between 1797 and 1809, the [First French Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_French_Empire) increasingly occupied the eastern [Adriatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adriatic) coastline and its hinterland, ending the Venetian and the [Ragusan republics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Ragusa), establishing the [Illyrian Provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_Provinces).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005422–423-52] In response, the [Royal Navy](26061.md) [blockaded the Adriatic Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blockade_of_the_Adriatic_Sea), leading to the [Battle of Vis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lissa_\(1811\)) in 1811.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdkinsAdkins2008359–362-57] The Illyrian provinces were captured by the Austrians in 1813 and absorbed by the [Austrian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austrian_Empire) following the [Congress of Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_Vienna) in 1815. This led to the formation of the [Kingdom of Dalmatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Dalmatia) and the restoration of the [Croatian Littoral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Littoral) to the Kingdom of Croatia under one crown.[^cite_note-58] The 1830s and 1840s featured [romantic nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romantic_nationalism) that inspired the [Croatian National Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_National_Revival), a political and cultural campaign advocating the unity of [South Slavs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Slavs) within the empire. Its primary focus was establishing a standard language as a counterweight to [Hungarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_language) while promoting [Croatian literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_literature) and culture.[^cite_note-CRIS-Stančić-59] During the [Hungarian Revolution of 1848](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1848), Croatia sided with Austria. Ban [Josip Jelačić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josip_Jela%C4%8Di%C4%87) helped defeat the Hungarians in 1849 and ushered in a [Germanisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germanisation) policy.[^cite_note-60] This is around the time that [Nikola Tesla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikola_Tesla) was born in [Smiljan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smiljan,_Croatia).&#x20;

By the 1860s, the failure of the policy became apparent, leading to the [Austro-Hungarian Compromise of 1867](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Compromise_of_1867). The creation of a [personal union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personal_union) between the Austrian Empire and the [Kingdom of Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary) followed. The treaty left Croatia's status to Hungary, which was resolved by the [Croatian–Hungarian Settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian%E2%80%93Hungarian_Settlement) of 1868 when the kingdoms of Croatia and Slavonia were united.[^cite_note-61] The Kingdom of Dalmatia remained under de facto Austrian control, while [Rijeka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rijeka) retained the status of [*corpus separatum*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_separatum_\(Fiume\)) previously introduced in 1779.[^cite_note-HR-HU-Heka-49] After [Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria-Hungary) [occupied Bosnia and Herzegovina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_rule_in_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina) following the [1878 Treaty of Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1878_Treaty_of_Berlin), the Military Frontier was abolished. The Croatian and [Slavonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavonian_Military_Frontier) sectors of the Frontier returned to Croatia in 1881,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005422–423-52] under provisions of the Croatian–Hungarian Settlement.[^cite_note-62][^cite_note-Dubravica-63] Renewed efforts to [reform Austria-Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Austria-Hungary), entailing [federalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalisation) with Croatia as a federal unit, were stopped by [World War I](4764461.md).[^cite_note-64]&#x20;

### The World Wars and Yugoslavia

Main articles: [Creation of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Creation_of_Yugoslavia), [Kingdom of Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Yugoslavia), [Banovina of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banovina_of_Croatia), [World War II in Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II_in_Yugoslavia), and [Socialist Republic of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Croatia)



Mass protests in Zagreb against the unification of the [State of Slovenes, Croats and Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats_and_Serbs) with the [Kingdom of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia) in 1918

On 29 October 1918, the Croatian Parliament (*Sabor*) declared independence and decided to join the newly formed [State of Slovenes, Croats, and Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Slovenes,_Croats,_and_Serbs),[^cite_note-Povijest-saborovanja-50] which in turn entered into union with the [Kingdom of Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia) on 4 December 1918 to form the [Kingdom of Serbs, Croats, and Slovenes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbs,_Croats,_and_Slovenes).[^cite_note-65] The Croatian Parliament never ratified the union with Serbia and Montenegro.[^cite_note-Povijest-saborovanja-50] The [1921 constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1921_constitution) defining the country as a [unitary state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) and abolition of Croatian Parliament and historical administrative divisions effectively ended Croatian autonomy.&#x20;

The new constitution was opposed by the most widely supported national political party—the [Croatian Peasant Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Peasant_Party) (HSS) led by [Stjepan Radić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stjepan_Radi%C4%87).[^cite_note-66] The political situation deteriorated further as Radić was assassinated in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_Parliament) by [NRS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People%27s_Radical_Party) member, [Serbian](59512.md) nationalist politician [Puniša Račić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puni%C5%A1a_Ra%C4%8Di%C4%87) in 1928, culminating in King [Alexander I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_I_of_Yugoslavia)'s establishment of the [6 January Dictatorship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/6_January_Dictatorship) in 1929.[^cite_note-67] The dictatorship formally ended in 1931 when the king imposed a more unitary constitution.[^cite_note-68] The HSS, now led by [Vladko Maček](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladko_Ma%C4%8Dek), continued to advocate federalisation, resulting in the [Cvetković–Maček Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cvetkovi%C4%87%E2%80%93Ma%C4%8Dek_Agreement) of August 1939 and the autonomous [Banovina of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banovina_of_Croatia). The Yugoslav government retained control of defence, internal security, foreign affairs, trade, and transport while other matters were left to the Croatian Sabor and a crown-appointed Ban.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlemenčičŽagar2004121–123-69]&#x20;



People of Zagreb celebrating [liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zagreb_in_World_War_II) on 12 May 1945 by [Croatian Partisans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Partisans)

In April 1941, [Yugoslavia was invaded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_Yugoslavia) by [Nazi Germany](21212.md) and [Fascist Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fascist_Italy_\(1922%E2%80%931943\)). Following the invasion, a German-Italian installed puppet state named the [Independent State of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_State_of_Croatia) (NDH) was established. Most of Croatia, Bosnia and Herzegovina, and the region of [Syrmia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrmia) were incorporated into this state. Parts of Dalmatia were [annexed by Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaties_of_Rome_\(1941\)), Hungary annexed the northern Croatian regions of [Baranja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baranya_\(region\)) and [Međimurje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me%C4%91imurje_\(region\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlemenčičŽagar2004153–156-70] The NDH regime was led by [Ante Pavelić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ante_Paveli%C4%87) and the ultranationalist [Ustaše](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usta%C5%A1e), a fringe movement in pre-war Croatia.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETomasevich2001337-71] With German and Italian military and political support,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETomasevich2001272-72] the regime introduced [racial laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_racial_theories) and launched a [genocide campaign against Serbs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_of_Serbs_in_the_Independent_State_of_Croatia), [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Croatia), and [Roma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people_in_Croatia).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlemenčičŽagar2004184-73] Many were imprisoned in [concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentration_camps_in_the_Independent_State_of_Croatia); the largest was the [Jasenovac complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasenovac_concentration_camp).[^cite_note-logori-74] [Anti-fascist Croats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Partisans) were targeted by the regime as well.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldstein1999138-75] Several [concentration camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_concentration_camps) (most notably the [Rab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rab_concentration_camp), [Gonars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonars_concentration_camp) and [Molat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molat_concentration_camp) camps) were established in Italian-occupied territories, mostly for [Slovenes](246311.md) and Croats.[^cite_note-logori-74] At the same time, the Yugoslav Royalist and [Serbian nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbian_nationalism) [Chetniks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chetniks) pursued a [genocidal campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chetnik_war_crimes_in_World_War_II) against Croats and [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosniaks),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlemenčičŽagar2004184-73][^cite_note-76] aided by Italy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETomasevich2001744-77] Nazi German forces committed crimes and reprisals against civilians in retaliation for Partisan actions, such as in the villages of [Kamešnica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre_of_villages_under_Kame%C5%A1nica) and [Lipa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_Centre_Lipa_Remembers) in 1944.[^cite_note-78][^cite_note-79]&#x20;

&#x20;A [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Liberation_Movement_in_Croatia) emerged. On 22 June 1941,[^cite_note-Pavličević-80] the [1st Sisak Partisan Detachment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Sisak_Partisan_Detachment) was formed near [Sisak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sisak), the first military unit formed by a resistance movement in [occupied Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German-occupied_Europe).[^cite_note-pavlicevic-2007-81] That sparked the beginning of the [Yugoslav Partisan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_Partisan) movement, a communist, multi-ethnic anti-fascist resistance group led by [Josip Broz Tito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito).[^cite_note-82] In ethnic terms, Croats were the second-largest contributors to the Partisan movement after Serbs.[^cite_note-Hoare_2011-83] In per capita terms, Croats contributed proportionately to their population within Yugoslavia.[^cite_note-84] By May 1944 (according to Tito), Croats made up 30% of the Partisan's ethnic composition, despite making up 22% of the population.[^cite_note-Hoare_2011-83] The movement grew fast, and at the [Tehran Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran_Conference) in December 1943, the Partisans gained recognition from the [Allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II).[^cite_note-85]



Cardinal [Aloysius Stepinac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aloysius_Stepinac) with the Croatian communist leader [Vladimir Bakarić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Bakari%C4%87) at the celebration of [May Day](39353050.md) in 1945.

With Allied support in logistics, equipment, training and airpower, and with the assistance of [Soviet troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army) taking part in the 1944 [Belgrade Offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgrade_Offensive), the Partisans gained control of Yugoslavia and the border regions of [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trieste#World_War_II_and_aftermath) and [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carinthia_\(state\)#From_1920_to_the_Present) by May 1945. Members of the [NDH armed forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Armed_Forces_\(Independent_State_of_Croatia\)) and other Axis troops, as well as civilians, were in retreat towards Austria. Following their surrender, many were killed in the [Yugoslav death march of Nazi collaborators](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yugoslav_death_march_of_Nazi_collaborators).[^cite_note-86] In the following years, [ethnic Germans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germans_of_Croatia) faced [persecution in Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flight_and_expulsion_of_Germans_\(1944%E2%80%9350\)#Yugoslavia), and many were interned.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldstein1999158-87] The political aspirations of the Partisan movement were reflected in the [State Anti-fascist Council for the National Liberation of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Anti-fascist_Council_for_the_National_Liberation_of_Croatia), which developed in 1943 as the bearer of Croatian statehood and later transformed into the Parliament in 1945, and [AVNOJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AVNOJ)—its counterpart at the Yugoslav level.[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89]&#x20;

Based on the studies on [wartime and post-war casualties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II_in_Yugoslavia_casualties) by demographer [Vladimir Žerjavić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_%C5%BDerjavi%C4%87) and statistician [Bogoljub Kočović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogoljub_Ko%C4%8Dovi%C4%87), a total of 295,000 people from the territory (not including territories [ceded from Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Peace_with_Italy,_1947) after the war) died, which amounted to 7.3% of the population,[^cite_note-90] among whom were 125–137,000 Serbs, 118–124,000 Croats, 16–17,000 Jews, and 15,000 Roma.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEŽerjavić1992159-91][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKočović1985173-92] In addition, from areas joined to Croatia after the war, a total of 32,000 people died, among whom 16,000 were Italians and 15,000 were Croats.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEŽerjavić1993b640–641-93] Approximately 200,000 Croats from the entirety of Yugoslavia (including Croatia) and abroad were killed in total throughout the war and its immediate aftermath, approximately 5.4% of the population.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKočović1985126-94][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGeiger2012117–118-95]&#x20;



[Josip Broz Tito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josip_Broz_Tito) led [Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia) from 1944 to 1980; Pictured: Tito with the US president [Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon) in the [White House](33057.md), 1971

After [World War II](32927.md), Croatia became a [single-party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single-party) socialist [federal unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Republic_of_Croatia) of the [SFR Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Federal_Republic_of_Yugoslavia), ruled by the [Communists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Communists_of_Croatia), but having a degree of autonomy within the federation. In 1967, Croatian authors and linguists published a [Declaration on the Status and Name of the Croatian Standard Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_on_the_Status_and_Name_of_the_Croatian_Standard_Language) demanding equal treatment for their language.[^cite_note-Šute-Deklaracija-96] The declaration contributed to a national movement seeking greater civil rights and redistribution of the Yugoslav economy, culminating in the [Croatian Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Spring) of 1971, which was suppressed by Yugoslav leadership.[^cite_note-JL-Savka-97] Still, the [1974 Yugoslav Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Yugoslav_Constitution) gave increased autonomy to federal units, basically fulfilling a goal of the Croatian Spring and providing a legal basis for independence of the federative constituents.[^cite_note-Rich-98] At the same time, there was a substantial Croatian diaspora during the Cold War, which included efforts aimed at forming a government in exile.[^cite_note-Mišur-99]&#x20;

Following Tito's death in 1980, the political situation in Yugoslavia deteriorated. National tension was fanned by the 1986 [SANU Memorandum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SANU_Memorandum) and the [1989 coups in Vojvodina, Kosovo, and Montenegro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_coups_in_Vojvodina,_Kosovo_and_Montenegro).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrucht2005433-100][^cite_note-101] In January 1990, the Communist Party fragmented along national lines, with the Croatian [faction](40362512.md) demanding a looser federation.[^cite_note-Pauković-14Congress-2009-102] In the same year, the [first multi-party elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Croatian_parliamentary_election) were held in Croatia, while [Franjo Tuđman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franjo_Tu%C4%91man)'s win exacerbated nationalist tensions.[^cite_note-Independent-Tuđman-Obituary-103] Some of the [Serbs in Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbs_in_Croatia) left Sabor and declared autonomy of the unrecognised [Republic of Serbian Krajina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Serbian_Krajina), intent on achieving independence from Croatia.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Autonomy-AUG1990-104][^cite_note-EE-CIS-book-105]&#x20;

### Independence

Main articles: [Independence of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Croatia), [Croatian War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_War_of_Independence), and [History of Croatia since 1995](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Croatia_since_1995)



The Memorial Cemetery — the central site of the [National Remembrance Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remembrance_Day_\(Croatia\)), commemorating all war victims and the [Vukovar massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vukovar_massacre) in 1991

As tensions rose, Croatia [declared independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_of_Croatia) on 25 June 1991. The full implementation of the declaration only came into effect after a [three-month moratorium on the decision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brioni_Agreement) on 8 October 1991.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Declaration-26June1991-106][^cite_note-Sabor-Independence-8Oct1991-107] In the meantime, tensions escalated into [overt war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_War_of_Independence) when the Serbian-controlled [Yugoslav People's Army](536643.md) (JNA) and various Serb paramilitary groups attacked Croatia.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Otkos10-108] By the end of 1991, a high-intensity conflict fought along a wide front reduced Croatia's control to about two-thirds of its territory.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Iceland-109][^cite_note-LATimes-OccupiedPct-1Aug91-110] Serb paramilitary groups then began a campaign of killing, terror, and expulsion of the Croats in the occupied territories, killing thousands[^cite_note-111] of Croat civilians and expelling or displacing as many as 400,000–500,000 Croats and other non-Serbs from their homes.[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_2020-113] Serbs living in Croatian towns, especially those near the front lines, were subjected to various forms of discrimination.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldstein1999233-114] Croatian Serbs in Eastern and Western Slavonia and parts of the Krajina were forced to flee or were expelled by Croatian forces, though on a restricted scale and in lesser numbers.[^cite_note-115] The [Croatian Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Croatia) publicly deplored these practices and sought to stop them, indicating that they were not a part of the Government's policy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAllen199646-116]&#x20;

On 15 January 1992, Croatia gained [diplomatic recognition](524626.md) by the [European Economic Community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Economic_Community), followed by the United Nations.[^cite_note-NYTimes-Germany-23Dec91-117][^cite_note-NYT-UN-membership-118] The war effectively ended in August 1995 with a [decisive victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Storm) by Croatia;[^cite_note-LATimes-Storm-Complete-119] the event is commemorated each year on 5 August as [Victory and Homeland Thanksgiving Day and the Day of Croatian Defenders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Day_\(Croatia\)).[^cite_note-120] Following the Croatian victory, about 200,000 Serbs from the self-proclaimed [Republic of Serbian Krajina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_Serbian_Krajina) fled the region[^cite_note-bbc-storm-121] and hundreds of mainly elderly Serb civilians were killed in the aftermath of the military operation, often in revenge attacks, accompanied by looting, seizing or burning down of their property.[^cite_note-122] Approximately half have returned since then.[^cite_note-Al_Jazeera_2020-113] Some 19,000 Serb homes were subsequently settled by Croat refugees from Bosnia and Herzegovina.[^cite_note-123] The [remaining occupied areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Slavonia,_Baranja_and_Western_Syrmia_\(1995%E2%80%931998\)) were restored to Croatia following the [Erdut Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erdut_Agreement) of November 1995, concluding with the [UNTAES](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Transitional_Administration_for_Eastern_Slavonia,_Baranja_and_Western_Sirmium) mission in January 1998.[^cite_note-NYTimes-UNTAES-16Jan98-124] Most sources number the war deaths at around 20,000.[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-setimes1-126][^cite_note-tehrantimes1-127]&#x20;



Croatia joined the [EU](9317.md) in 2013 and the [Eurozone](184391.md) a decade later in 2023.

At the turn of the 21st century, Croatia experienced sustained economic growth as the nation democratized but struggled with public corruption and bouts of unemployment.[^cite_note-128] The Croatian Parliament passed a set of constitutional amendments in November 2000 and March 2001 to convert from a bicameral to a unicameral body, later limiting presidential powers.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] Croatia joined the [Partnership for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace) in May 2000 and the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) in November 2000.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] It joined the military alliance [NATO](21133.md) in April 2009.[^cite_note-133] Croatia [was elected](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_United_Nations_Security_Council_election) to serve on the non-permanent seat of the [UN Security Council](31956.md) from 2008 to 2009.[^cite_note-MVPEI-UNSC-2008-2009-134] Adversely impacted by the [2008 financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_financial_crisis),[^cite_note-135] austerity measures led to widespread anti-government protests in 2009 and 2011.[^cite_note-136] In the latter year, parliament was dissolved in May, with Croatian president [Ivo Josipović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivo_Josipovi%C4%87) calling for new elections to quell the protests by December.[^cite_note-137][^cite_note-138] After successfully completing [negotiations from 2004 to 2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_enlargement_of_the_European_Union),[^cite_note-EU-Negotiations-Completed-139][^cite_note-140] Croatia joined the [European Union](9317.md) (EU) as its 28th member in July 2013.[^cite_note-EU-Accession-Treaty-141]&#x20;

Croatia was affected by the [2015 European migrant crisis](46415102.md) when Hungary's closure of borders with Serbia pushed over 700,000 refugees and migrants through Croatia.[^cite_note-142] On 19 October 2016, [Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87) began serving as the current Croatian Prime Minister.[^cite_note-143] The most recent presidential elections, held on 12 January 2025, elected [Zoran Milanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoran_Milanovi%C4%87) as president.[^cite_note-144] In 2022, Croatia began the process to join the [OECD](33853117.md), due to complete membership proceedings by 2026.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146] The nation joined the [Eurozone](184391.md) and [Schengen Area](16565139.md) in 2023, adopting the [euro](9472.md) as its official currency.[^cite_note-147]&#x20;

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Croatia)



[Satellite image of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Croatia)

Croatia is situated in [Central](5188.md) and Southeast Europe, on the coast of the [Adriatic Sea](23275478.md). Hungary is to the northeast, [Serbia](29265.md) to the east, [Bosnia and Herzegovina](3463.md) and [Montenegro](20760.md) to the southeast and [Slovenia](27338.md) to the northwest. It lies mostly between latitudes [42°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/42nd_parallel_north) and [47° N](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/47%C2%B0_N) and longitudes [13°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/13th_meridian_east) and [20° E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20%C2%B0_E).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2018_Statistical_Yearbook_of_the_Republic_of_Croatia44-148] Part of the territory in the extreme south surrounding [Dubrovnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubrovnik) is a [practical exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Practical_exclave) connected to the rest of the mainland by [territorial waters](496983.md), but separated on land by a short coastline strip belonging to Bosnia and Herzegovina around [Neum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neum). The [Pelješac Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pelje%C5%A1ac_Bridge) connects the exclave with mainland Croatia.[^cite_note-149]&#x20;

The territory covers 56,594 square kilometres (21,851 square miles), consisting of 56,414 square kilometres (21,782 square miles) of land and 128 square kilometres (49 square miles) of water. It is the world's 127th largest country.[^cite_note-CIA-150] Elevation ranges from the mountains of the [Dinaric Alps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinaric_Alps) with the highest point of the [Dinara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinara) peak at 1,831 metres (6,007 feet) near the border with Bosnia and Herzegovina in the south to the shore of the Adriatic Sea which makes up its entire southwest border.[^cite_note-CIA-150] Insular Croatia consists of over [a thousand islands and islets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_thousand_islands_and_islets) varying in size, 48 of which are [permanently inhabited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_inhabited_islands_of_Croatia).[^cite_note-\:7-151] The largest islands are [Cres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cres) and [Krk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krk), each of them having an area of around 405 square kilometres (156 square miles).[^cite_note-CIA-150] It is the [largest overseas territory](23275478.md#Geography) on the Adriatic Sea.[^cite_note-\:7-151]&#x20;



[Telašćica Nature Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tela%C5%A1%C4%87ica_Nature_Park)



[Tourist cruise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_cruise) on the [Danube](21209639.md) river, eastern [Slavonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavonia)

The hilly northern parts of [Hrvatsko Zagorje](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hrvatsko_Zagorje) and the flat plains of Slavonia in the east which is part of the [Pannonian Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pannonian_Basin) are traversed by major rivers such as [Danube](21209639.md), [Drava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drava), [Kupa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupa), and the [Sava](587845.md). The Danube, Europe's second longest river, runs through the city of [Vukovar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vukovar) in the extreme east and forms part of the border with Serbia. The central and southern regions near the Adriatic coastline and islands consist of low mountains and forested highlands. Natural resources found in quantities significant enough for production include oil, coal, bauxite, low-grade iron ore, calcium, gypsum, natural asphalt, silica, mica, clays, salt, and hydropower.[^cite_note-CIA-150] [Karst topography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karst_topography) makes up about half of Croatia and is especially prominent in the Dinaric Alps.[^cite_note-152] Croatia hosts [deep caves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_caves_in_Croatia), 49 of which are deeper than 250 m (820.21 ft), 14 deeper than 500 m (1,640.42 ft) and three deeper than 1,000 m (3,280.84 ft). The [Plitvice lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plitvice_Lakes_National_Park) are a system of 16 lakes with waterfalls connecting them over [dolomite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolomite_\(mineral\)) and [limestone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limestone) cascades. The lakes are known for their distinctive colours, ranging from turquoise to mint green, grey or blue.[^cite_note-BBC-Plitvice-153]&#x20;

### Climate



[Köppen-Geiger climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) map of Croatia

Most of Croatia has a moderately warm and rainy [continental climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_climate) as defined by the [Köppen climate classification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification).[^cite_note-\:8-154] Mean monthly temperature ranges between −3 [°C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celsius) (27 [°F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahrenheit)) in January and 18 °C (64 °F) in July. The coldest parts of the country are [Lika](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lika) and [Gorski Kotar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorski_Kotar) featuring a snowy, forested climate at elevations above 1,200 metres (3,900 feet).[^cite_note-\:8-154] The warmest areas are at the Adriatic coast and especially in its immediate hinterland characterised by [Mediterranean climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_climate), as the sea moderates temperature highs.[^cite_note-\:8-154] Consequently, temperature peaks are more pronounced in continental areas.[^cite_note-\:8-154]&#x20;

The lowest temperature of −35.5 °C (−31.9 °F) was recorded on 3 February 1919 in [Čakovec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%8Cakovec),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2018_Statistical_Yearbook_of_the_Republic_of_Croatia40-155] and the highest temperature of 42.8 °C (109.0 °F) was recorded on 4 August 1981 in [Ploče](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plo%C4%8De).[^cite_note-record_temp-156] Mean annual precipitation ranges between 600 millimetres (24 inches) and 3,500 millimetres (140 inches) depending on geographic region and climate type. The least precipitation is recorded in the outer islands ([Biševo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bi%C5%A1evo), [Lastovo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lastovo), [Svetac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svetac), [Vis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vis_\(island\))) and the eastern parts of Slavonia. However, in the latter case, rain occurs mostly during the [growing season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Growing_season). The maximum precipitation levels are observed in the Dinaric Alps, in the Gorski Kotar peaks of [Risnjak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Risnjak) and [Snježnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snje%C5%BEnik).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2018_Statistical_Yearbook_of_the_Republic_of_Croatia40-155]&#x20;

Prevailing winds in the interior are light to moderate northeast or southwest, and in the coastal area, prevailing winds are determined by local features. Higher wind velocities are more often recorded in cooler months along the coast, generally as the cool northeasterly [bura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora_\(wind\)) or less frequently as the warm southerly [jugo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jugo). The sunniest parts are the outer islands, Hvar and Korčula, where more than 2700 hours of sunshine are recorded per year, followed by the middle and southern Adriatic Sea area in general, and northern Adriatic coast, all with more than 2,000 hours of sunshine per year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTE2018_Statistical_Yearbook_of_the_Republic_of_Croatia41-157]&#x20;

### Biodiversity

Main article: [Protected areas of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Croatia)

Further information: [Register of Protected Natural Values of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Register_of_Protected_Natural_Values_of_Croatia) and [Natural and Cultural Heritage of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_and_Cultural_Heritage_of_Croatia)



[Plitvice Lakes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plitvice_Lakes_National_Park)



Landscapes of [Motovun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motovun) in [Istrian peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istria_County)



[Galešnjak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gale%C5%A1njak) island in a shape of a heart

Croatia can be subdivided into [ecoregions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion) based on climate and geomorphology. The country is one of the richest in Europe in terms of biodiversity.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] Croatia has four types of biogeographical regions—the [Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Biogeographic_Region) along the coast and in its immediate hinterland, [Alpine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alpine_Biogeographic_Region) in most of Lika and Gorski Kotar, [Pannonian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pannonian_Biogeographic_Region) along Drava and Danube, and [Continental](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Biogeographic_Region) in the remaining areas. The most significant are [karst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karst) habitats which include submerged karst, such as [Zrmanja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zrmanja) and [Krka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krka_\(Croatia\)) canyons and tufa barriers, as well as underground habitats. The country contains three ecoregions: [Dinaric Mountains mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests), [Pannonian mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pannonian_mixed_forests), and [Illyrian deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_deciduous_forests).[^cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-160]&#x20;

The karst geology harbours approximately [7,000 caves and pits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_caves_in_Croatia), some of which are the habitat of the only known aquatic cave [vertebrate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertebrate)—the [olm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olm). Forests are abundant, covering 2,490,000 hectares (6,200,000 acres) or 44% of Croatian land area. Other habitat types include wetlands, grasslands, bogs, fens, scrub habitats, coastal and marine habitats.[^cite_note-MCult-Biodiversity-161]&#x20;

In terms of [phytogeography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phytogeography), Croatia is a part of the [Boreal Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom) and is a part of Illyrian and Central European provinces of the [Circumboreal Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circumboreal_Region) and the Adriatic province of the [Mediterranean Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Region). The [World Wide Fund for Nature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature) divides Croatia between three ecoregions—Pannonian mixed forests, [Dinaric Mountains mixed forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dinaric_Mountains_mixed_forests) and [Illyrian deciduous forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Illyrian_deciduous_forests).[^cite_note-DU-Sustdev-162]&#x20;

Croatia hosts 37,000 known plant and animal species, but their actual number is estimated to be between 50,000 and 100,000.[^cite_note-MCult-Biodiversity-161] More than a thousand species are endemic, especially in Velebit and Biokovo mountains, Adriatic islands and karst rivers. Legislation protects 1,131 species.[^cite_note-MCult-Biodiversity-161] The most serious threat is habitat loss and degradation. A further problem is presented by invasive alien species, especially *[Caulerpa taxifolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caulerpa_taxifolia)* algae.&#x20;

Invasive algae are regularly monitored and removed to protect [benthic habitat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benthic_habitat). Indigenous cultivated plant strains and domesticated animal breeds are numerous. They include five breeds of horses, five of cattle, eight of sheep, two of pigs, and one poultry. Indigenous breeds include nine that are endangered or critically endangered.[^cite_note-MCult-Biodiversity-161] Croatia has 411 [protected areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Croatia), encompassing 9% of the country. Those include eight [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_park), two [strict reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strict_nature_reserve), and thirteen [nature parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_park). The oldest national park in country is [Plitvice Lakes National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plitvice_Lakes_National_Park), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Sites_in_Croatia). Velebit Nature Park is a part of the UNESCO [Man and the Biosphere Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_and_the_Biosphere_Programme). The strict and special reserves, as well as the national and nature parks, are managed and protected by the central government, while other protected areas are managed by counties. In 2005, the National Ecological Network was set up, as the first step in the preparation of the EU accession and joining of the [Natura 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natura_2000) network.[^cite_note-MCult-Biodiversity-161]&#x20;

## Governance

Further information: [Politics of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Croatia) and [Human rights in Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Croatia)

Zoran Milanović

[Zoran Milanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoran_Milanovi%C4%87)\
[President](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Croatia)

Andrej Plenković

[Andrej Plenković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Plenkovi%C4%87)\
[Prime Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Croatia)

The Republic of Croatia is a unitary constitutional republic using a [parliamentary system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_system). Government powers in Croatia are legislative, executive, and judiciary powers.[^cite_note-VRH-PoliticalStructure-163] The [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Croatia) is the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state), directly elected to a five-year term and is limited by the constitution to two terms. In addition to serving as [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief) of the armed forces, the president has the procedural duty of appointing the prime minister with the parliament and has some influence on foreign policy.[^cite_note-VRH-PoliticalStructure-163]&#x20;

The [government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Government) is headed by the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Croatia), who is supported by [deputy prime ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deputy_Prime_Minister_of_Croatia) and various [ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Croatia#Ministries) in charge of particular sectors.[^cite_note-Govt-Ministers-164] As the [executive branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_\(government\)), it is responsible for proposing legislation and a budget, enforcing the laws, and guiding foreign and internal policies. The government is seated at [Banski dvori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banski_dvori) in Zagreb.[^cite_note-VRH-PoliticalStructure-163]&#x20;

### Law and judicial system

Further information: [Law of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Croatia)



[Croatian Parliament](398696.md) (Sabor) in Zagreb



[Banski dvori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banski_dvori) in Zagreb, seat of the [Government of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Croatia)

A [unicameral parliament](204304.md) – known as the *_Sabor_* – holds [legislative power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_power). The number of parliament members can vary from 100 to 160. They are elected by popular vote to serve four-year terms. Legislative sessions take place from 15 January to 15 July, and from 15 September to 15 December annually.[^cite_note-Sabor-about-165] The two largest [political parties in Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Croatia) are the [Croatian Democratic Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Democratic_Union) (HDZ) and the [Social Democratic Party of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Democratic_Party_of_Croatia) (SDP).[^cite_note-Sabor-MPs-166]&#x20;

Croatia has a [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) legal system in which law arises primarily from written statutes, with judges serving as implementers and not creators of law. Its development was largely influenced by German and Austrian legal systems. Croatian law is divided into two principal areas—[private](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_law) and [public law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_law). Before [EU accession negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_enlargement_of_the_European_Union) were completed, Croatian legislation had been fully harmonised with the [community acquis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Community_acquis).[^cite_note-DELHRV-overview-167]&#x20;

The main national courts are the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Croatia), which oversees violations of the Constitution, and the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Croatia), which is the highest court of appeal. A variety of administrative, commercial, [county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_Court), misdemeanor, and municipal courts handle cases in their respective domains.[^cite_note-VSRH-about-168] Cases falling within judicial jurisdiction are in the first instance decided by a single professional judge, while appeals are deliberated in mixed tribunals of professional judges. Lay magistrates also participate in trials.[^cite_note-169] The [State's Attorney Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State%27s_Attorney_Office_of_the_Republic_of_Croatia) is the judicial body constituted of public prosecutors empowered to instigate prosecution of perpetrators of offences.[^cite_note-170] Law enforcement agencies are organised under the authority of the [Ministry of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_the_Interior_\(Croatia\)) which consist primarily of the national police force. Croatia's security service is the [Security and Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_and_Intelligence_Agency_\(Croatia\)) (SOA).[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Croatia)



President [Zoran Milanović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoran_Milanovi%C4%87) with other Western leaders at the [2025 NATO Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit)

Croatia has established diplomatic relations with most world nations – [189 states in total](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_relations_of_Croatia) – starting [with Germany](18825361.md) (1991) and most recently [with Liberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Liberia) (2024).[^cite_note-173] It exerts influence as a [small power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_power) in this region and within European international institutions.[^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] Since the 1990s, Croatia has had unresolved border disputes throughout Southeast Europe, namely [with Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia%E2%80%93Serbia_border_dispute), Bosnia and Herzegovina, Montenegro, and Slovenia.[^cite_note-Border_issues-176] It is a member of the [United Nations](31769.md) (UN),[^cite_note-177] the [European Union](9317.md),[^cite_note-BBC-Croatia-EU-178] [NATO](21133.md),[^cite_note-NYTimes-Bush-Zagreb-179] the [Council of Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Europe),[^cite_note-180] the [Union for the Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_for_the_Mediterranean),[^cite_note-181] and the [Schengen Area](16565139.md) as well as the [Eurozone](184391.md).[^cite_note-BBC-Croatia-EU-178][^cite_note-BBC-Croatia-NATO-182] Modern Croatian foreign policy objectives include enhancing relations with neighbouring countries, developing international cooperation and promotion of the Croatian economy.[^cite_note-MVPEI-Goals-183]&#x20;

It supports 57 embassies, 30 consulates and eight permanent diplomatic missions. Many international organisations have regional offices in Croatia, such as the [European Bank for Reconstruction and Development](37963.md) (EBRD), [International Organization for Migration](36893.md) (IOM), [Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe](22796.md) (OSCE), [World Bank](36752.md), [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO), [International Criminal Tribunal for the former Yugoslavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Tribunal_for_the_former_Yugoslavia) (ICTY), [United Nations Development Programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Development_Programme) (UNDP), [United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees](31964.md) (UNHCR), and [UNICEF](19698110.md).[^cite_note-184]&#x20;

### Croatian diaspora

Main article: [Croatian diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_diaspora)

The diaspora consists of communities of ethnic [Croats](69801.md) and [Croatian citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_nationality_law) living outside Croatia. The [largest diaspora is in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Americans), with a large population across neighboring Southeast Europe.[^cite_note-185] The country maintains extensive contact with Croatian communities abroad through administrative and financial support of cultural, sports activities, and economic initiatives.[^cite_note-186] Croatia actively maintains foreign relations to strengthen and guarantee the rights of the Croatian minority abroad.[^cite_note-187][^cite_note-188]&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Armed Forces of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_of_Croatia)

Further information: [Defence industry of Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_industry_of_Croatia)



[Croatian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Army)



[Croatian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Navy)



[Croatian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Air_Force)

The Armed Forces of Croatia (OSRH) consists of three service branches: the [Croatian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Army), the [Croatian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Navy) and the [Croatian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_Air_Force). They are headed by the President of Croatia as commander-in-chief while defence policy is overseen by the Government of Croatia.[^cite_note-CAFstructure-189] The military supports the foreign policy of Croatia and international security initiatives. It is the sole military wing of the [Military Security and Intelligence Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_Security_and_Intelligence_Agency_\(Croatia\)) (VSOA). The Armed Forces are spread across [continental Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Croatia) and capital city Zagreb, with the Navy largely based in [Split](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Split,_Croatia). According to the 2025 [Global Peace Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Peace_Index), Croatia is the 18th most peaceful country in the world.[^cite_note-190]&#x20;

Since the [Croatian War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatian_War_of_Independence) (1991–1995), the Armed Forces have played a key role in protecting territorial integrity. The military's first foreign deployment was in 2003 – the [War in Afghanistan](19666611.md) and [Iraq War](5043324.md) – later joining the military alliance [NATO](21133.md) in 2009.[^cite_note-\:9-191] Croatia maintains a close security and foreign intelligence relationship [with NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia%E2%80%93NATO_relations) and [the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia%E2%80%93United_States_relations#Military_cooperation).[^cite_note-192] The military has been deployed to various [United Nations](31769.md) (UN), NATO and American-led missions around the world.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195] Since joining the European Union (EU) in 2013, it became a part of the [EU defence forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_forces_of_the_European_Union).&#x20;

The Armed Forces have seen significant militarization and modernization since the early 2020s due to regional geopolitical risk.[^cite_note-196] Its deep integration within Europe and the U.S. has led to modern defence pacts and a robust [defence industrial base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_industrial_base).[^cite_note-197] The Croatian military is among the most [technologically advanced in Southeast Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_level_of_military_equipment).[^cite_note-\:2-198][^cite_note-\:3-199] Croatia has had [military conscription](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_Croatia) since 2025, previously enacted from 1991 to 2008.[^cite_note-vojnirokkraci-200][^cite_note-CIA-150]&#x20;
